Original problem, symbols, and uniform scalar guards\n\nFor a finite prescribed nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum\nof2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over arbitrary finite Euclidean\ndimensions, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an attained minimum,\na selected minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ORIGINAL updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The unchanged project target is the\nasymptotic exact minimax comparison to the original all-split Zhang--Jiang\nbenchmark, not a comparison within this coefficient family. The conditional\nroute in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a full-function upper bound\nW_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536.\n\nFix such an m throughout. All child words use these same parameters:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    b=1+a, ggap=1+beta,\n    H_0=(), H_(u+1)=(H_u,1+a*c^u,H_u),\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1, jtail=l-2,\n    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q).\n\nThe letter pi here is the new terminal optimum probability; it is NEVER\nidentified with1/(1+S). V will denote a clock moment, not a function value.\ne is the all-ones column in R^n, and e_i is its ith coordinate column.\nThe chronological matrix and clocks are\n\n    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_iu=h_u for u<i, Ltri_iu=0 for u>i,\n    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(u<i)h_u, T=t_l=S-a,\n    Delta=T-1=S-b.\n\nAll clocks are strictly increasing. The last three physical steps are\n(h_jtail,h_r,h_l)=(b,ggap,b), so T-t_r=ggap.\n\nWe will use the following intentionally loose exact enclosures:\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1,\n    41/100<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,\n    7/5<b<17/12, 19/10<ggap<2,\n    S>n>2^20, S<c^m, 0<pi<1/1000,\n    17/(48S)<q<17/(32S), D/S<201/100.                 (2.1)\n\nFor example sqrt(2)<99/70 and eps<=1/6144 give\n\n    a>(70/169)*(6143/6145)>41/100.\n\nThe latter rational inequality follows by positive cross multiplication.\nThe upper bound uses beta/(1+eps)<1 and sqrt(2)<17/12. The bounds on c and\nbeta follow directly from sqrt(2)>7/5 and eps<=1/6144. Every step is greater\nthan1, giving S>n. Put vword=a/(c-2). The inequalities c>1+sqrt(2) and\nbeta<1 give0<vword<1. Summing the word gives the exact finite identity\n\n    1+S_u=(1-vword)*2^u+vword*c^u.\n\nIt proves S<c^m. The q bounds follow from2S<D<3S; S>2^20 proves the stated\npi bound, and S>100 proves D/S<201/100. These are symbolic inequalities;\nno recurrence or numerical evaluation has been executed.\n\nFor a suffix length Ls in{0,...,n}, define its product\n\n    Rtail_Ls=product_(u=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_u-1), Rtail_0=1,\n    s=Rtail_n, k_i=h_i product_(i<u<n)(1-h_u).\n\nIf pop(Ls) is the binary digit sum, then\n\n    Rtail_Ls=beta^Ls*c^(-pop(Ls)),\n    |k_i|=Rtail_(l-i)+Rtail_(l-i+1)<=2,\n    k_i=(-1)^i |k_i|,\n    k_0=(b/a)s, k_l=b, k_r=-a*ggap, k_jtail=b*a*beta.   (2.2)\n\nIndeed h_(n-v)-1=a*c^v2(v), where v2(v) is the exponent of2 dividing v.\nCounting the multiples of each power of2 gives\nsum_(v=1)^Ls v2(v)=Ls-pop(Ls), proving the product formula. Multiplication\nby h_i=1+(h_i-1) proves the two-suffix formula for |k_i|. The parity is\nn-1-i, which has the parity of i since n is odd.\n\nFor m>=16, induction gives n>16m^2. Also\nbeta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>2 by the binomial expansion. Therefore\nbeta^n<2^(-4m), and\n\n    s*S^2 < beta^n*c^m < (5/32)^m < 1/64.\n\nTogether with(2.1), this proves0<s<pi<1. Define\n\n    E=pi+s, F=pi-s, eta=F/E.\n\nThen0<eta<1, pi<E<2pi, and eta*E^2=pi^2-s^2. In particular every\ndenominator E,a,pi,1-pi,Delta used below is strictly positive.\n\nThe finite products telescope to\n\n    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.                   (2.3)\n\nFor detail: k_i=h_i prod_(u>i)(1-h_u), and summing the corresponding\nsuccessive-product differences over any suffix gives\nsum_(u>=i)k_u=1-prod_(u>=i)(1-h_u). Thus\nk_i+h_i sum_(u>i)k_u=h_i, proving the middle identity; the full-sum\nidentity uses prod_(u<n)(1-h_u)=-s. Multiply the middle identity by e\nfor the clock identity. None of these identities substitutes a probability\nfor the actual suffix scalar s.\n\n## 3. Law I: literal terminal pool on actual adjacent clocks\n\nSet\n\n    V=(1-pi)/pi, tbar=(S-V)/(1-pi).\n\nA direct use of S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2 gives\n\n    (1-pi)(T-tbar)=15/(32pi)-b+(a+1/2)pi.\n\nUsing(2.1), the right side lies strictly between1/(3pi) and\n(1-pi)/(2pi). For the lower bound, subtract1/(3pi): the remaining\n13/(96pi)-b+(a+1/2)pi is positive. For the upper bound, subtract the\nright side from(1-pi)/(2pi): it is1/(32pi)+b-1/2-(a+1/2)pi>0.\nConsequently the weaker convenient bounds are\n\n    1/(3pi)<T-tbar<1/(2pi), tbar>S/2.                (3.1)\n\nThe last inequality follows from T=S-a and\nS/2>a+1/(2pi), using S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2 and pi<1/1000.\n\nThe first and last H_4 blocks have total mass S_4<c^4<40. Hence tbar\nis later than every clock in the first H_4 block and earlier than every\nclock in the last H_4 block: S/2>40 and T-tbar>1/(3pi)>40 suffice.\nIn particular the pool below avoids indices0,1 and jtail,r,l by a wide\nstrict margin. This uses actual original clocks, not a continuous-time\nruler approximation.\n\nLet j be the smallest original index such that t_(j+1)>=tbar. Set\n\n    alpha_j=(1-pi)(t_(j+1)-tbar)/(t_(j+1)-t_j),\n    alpha_(j+1)=(1-pi)(tbar-t_j)/(t_(j+1)-t_j),\n    alpha_i=0 at every other original query.\n\nThe denominator is exactly h_j>0. Both coefficients are nonnegative,\ne^T alpha=1-pi, and alpha^T t=S-V. If tbar=t_k exactly, then j=k-1\nand this formula puts the entire mass1-pi at k, with alpha_(k-1)=0.\nThere is no ambiguous tie choice or feasibility oracle. Put\n\n    v=k-alpha.\n\nThen e^T v=E and t^T v=V. Under Law I, in particular,\n\n    alpha_0=alpha_1=alpha_jtail=alpha_r=alpha_l=0,\n    v_0=bs/a, v_jtail=ba beta, v_r=-a ggap, v_l=b. Common column, physical clock metric, and every local detail\n\nThe common column is exactly the assigned source column:\n\n    z=(E/a)(b e_0-e_1), e^T z=E,\n    Z=t^T z=-E/a,\n    Xreq=D-pi^(-2)-eta Z^2\n        =D/17-(pi^2-s^2)/a^2.                         (4.1)\n\nHere t_0=1,t_1=1+b, so the stated Z follows by subtraction. Since a>2/5,\n\n    Xreq>D/17-425/(64D)>0\n\nas D>11. Also Xreq<D/17<S/5. The charge q=pi^2 is spent once; there is\nno paid vector zeta and no omitted initial derivative direction.\n\nWrite Hvec_i=h_i and define the finite physical-mass moments\n\n    Qmass=sum h_i^2, Rmass=sum h_i^3,\n    t_mass=sum h_i t_i/S,\n    Vmass=sum h_i(t_i-t_mass)^2,\n    rho_clock=Xreq/Vmass,\n    Kclock=rho_clock[diag(Hvec)-Hvec Hvec^T/S].         (4.2)\n\nEvery moment is given explicitly by the finite level-count formula\n\n    sum_(i<n) h_i^d = sum_(u=0)^(m-1) 2^(m-1-u)(1+a c^u)^d,\n    d=1,2,3.\n\nThis is a closed finite expression, not an evaluated recurrence. The word\nis palindromic. With P_i=t_i-1 its prefix mass, palindrome reversal sends\nP_i to S-P_i-h_i while retaining h_i. Thus\n\n    sum h_i P_i=(S^2-Qmass)/2,\n    sum h_i^2 P_i=(S Qmass-Rmass)/2.\n\nExpanding (P_i+h_i)^3-P_i^3 and summing gives\nsum h_i P_i^2=S^3/3-S Qmass/2+Rmass/6. Hence\n\n    t_mass=1+(S^2-Qmass)/(2S),\n    Vmass=S^3/12+Rmass/6-Qmass^2/(4S). Therefore\n\n    Qmass/S^2 < 2/S+(100/99)^2*(c-2)^2/(c^2-2)\n               < 1/500+1/16 < 1/15.\n\nThe middle inequality uses S>1000 and the fact that\n(c-2)^2/(c^2-2) increases on2<c<5/2, where it is less than1/17;\n(100/99)^2/17<1/16 by positive cross multiplication. Nonnegative last-row coefficients require\n\n    ggap*rlast <= Bl <= Delta*rlast.\n\nThe lower inequality follows from Bl>2b/(3pi)>26>ggap*rlast.\nFor the upper inequality, equivalently\n(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*Dlast<0, use\n2(Kt)_l<1, 2Vb<3/pi, Delta>S/2 and Dlast>1. Then the left side is\nless than1+3/pi-S/2<0, using(2.1) and S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2.\nBoth complete endpoint row screens therefore pass.\n\nThe final adjacent-pair invariant follows from the last rows of Ltri^T:\n\n    N_rl+N_lr=2P_rl+ggap*Pll=:C2.\n\nIf all finite coefficients were nonnegative, C2<=0 would be necessary.\nHere its exact components are\n\n    C2_clock=rho_clock*ggap*b[1-(b+2)/S],\n    C2_detail=-tau*b[2b+ggap*(2-b)],\n    C2_v=ggap*b*(1-a), C2_z=0.\n\nThe clock term is positive but less than1/100. The v term is less than2.\nSince tau>8b^2/(b+ggap)^2>32/25 and b>7/5,\n|C2_detail|>2*(32/25)*(49/25)>5. Hence C2<-1: this cut passes.\n\nThe exact final-three-query identity, rederived in the original orientation,\nis\n\n    C3=2(P_jtail,l+P_rl)+(3-a beta)Pll-mu_l-nu_l-2alpha_l\n       =b*lambda_(r,l)\n          +sum_(i<jtail)[lambda_(l,i)+lambda_(i,l)].  (7.4)\n\nIndeed, in addition to the adjacent identity,\nN_jtail,l+N_l,jtail=2P_jtail,l+b N_rl-b beta Pll.\nInsert these two identities into the sum of the original last row and last\ncolumn, using their sums mu_l and nu_l+2alpha_l, respectively, to get(7.4).\nThus C3>=0 is necessary regardless of the completion.\n\nFor Law I all four parts are explicit:\n\n    C3_clock=rho_clock*b[(3-a beta)\n                       -((3-a beta)b+2(b+ggap))/S]>0,\n    C3_detail=tau*b^2(1-a beta)>0,\n    C3_v_and_mu=b[3+a-a beta(1-a)-2pi]>0,\n    C3_z=0.\n\nThe first bracket is positive by S>2^20, a<1/2, b<3/2, ggap<2.\nThe other two signs follow from0<a beta<1/2 and pi<1/1000.\nHence this final-three-query cut passes as well. The old negative cut for\nthe fixed V=2, late-common-column architecture has not been reused.\n\nFor clarity, EVERY queried column capacity for either law has the literal\nformula\n\n    cap_i=Nii-nu_i-2alpha_i\n         =Pii+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_ui+Acan_ui)-2alpha_i. Its failure is uniform for every assigned m.\nIt decisively refutes the frozen first law while all the checked scalar,\noptimum, endpoint, and late-cut conditions above remain true.\n\nIt would be incorrect to infer failure of all compatible skews from(8.1).\nAll completions are Acan+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. Exact scope and outcome\n\nFor all the parameters below, the candidate conclusions established jointly\nin this report are:\n\n1. Every sloping-side complete child, with all its original queries AND its\n   terminal in that same side cell, obeys the sharp coupled identity(12).\n   Its planar objective score is at most1/(1+2S_j), with the SAME enclosing\n   m in every child. Height and the normal component are never optimized\n   independently.\n2. The scalar interval-distance tube does not extend to the triangle even\n   through a legal complete H_1. Section6 gives exact finite parameters and\n   both actual projection guards.\n3. The scalar small-radius terminal capture fails for an actual complete\n   H_m, including a finite orthogonal T=1 lift with TRUE initial radius\n   less than d_m. Its score is still below1/D. Thus this is a failure of\n   an intermediate invariant, not a counterexample to the target bound.\n4. Every legal member of the single first-leaf upper-to-lower switch pattern\n   in Section8 has planar and finite-tilt score STRICTLY below1/D. This is\n   uniform in the triangle angle, size, start and all m>=1536. It is a\n   closed all-depth pattern result, not a cell list or a finite experiment.\n\nThe unrestricted all-start all-L,H triangle bound(1+C/m)/D with an absolute C\nhas NOT been proved. No all-depth strict witness above17/(16D) was obtained.\nRepeated switches, changes involving the base or its endpoints, and\nunrestricted returns remain outside the proved pattern. No full-W bound or\nresolution of PROBLEM.md follows. Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 requires a\nFULL-class upper bound; accepted factd8b59c7d92425121 does not reduce its\narbitrary polytopes to these triangles.\n\n## 2. Word, geometry, objective and notation\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536. Define\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, D_j=1+2S_j, S=S_m, D=D_m, n=2^m-1,\n    ell=1+a, p_j=product_(h in H_j)(h-1), p_0=1, Q=p_m^2.\n\nThe physical entries h_i, 0<=i<n, are exactly H_m in their original order.\nAll prefixes below are original prefixes; no occurrence is omitted. The\nquantity d_m is auxiliary, not an additional physical step. The symbol H_j\nfor a word is distinct from the positive triangle height H.\n\nLet L,H>0 be finite. In the plane define\n\n    K=conv{0,(L,H),(L,-H)}, alpha=(1,0),\n    phi(z)=min_(y in K)[alpha.y+||z-y||^2/2],\n    y(z)=P_K(z-alpha), g(z)=z-y(z),\n    F_h(z)=z-h*g(z),\n    Psi_j=the chronological composition along H_j.\n\nP_K denotes Euclidean projection. The original function has advertised\nsmoothness1. For any advertised smoothness M_sm>0, use f=M_sm*phi and\nsteps h_i/M_sm; the normalized scores are unchanged and remain W=2R.\n\nIntroduce the finite side length and its direction cosines:\n\n    Rside=sqrt(L^2+H^2), u=L/Rside, v=H/Rside,\n    u>0, v>0, u^2+v^2=1.\n\nFor a sign sigma in{+1,-1}, set\n\n    e_sigma=(u,sigma*v), n_sigma=(-v,sigma*u),\n    r_sigma=e_sigma.z, w_sigma=n_sigma.z.\n\nEach pair is orthonormal; n_sigma is the outward normal of that sloping\nside. In Section8 only, kappa=u^2-v^2 and omega=2u*v denote the relative\nrotation coefficients, so kappa^2+omega^2=1. The triangle has no other\nvertices, coordinates or freely selectable face data.\n\nThe elementary projection characterization is\n\n    y=P_K(z-alpha) iff\n    (z-alpha-y).(b-y)<=0 for every b in K.             (1)\n\nIt suffices to test the three vertices, by linearity in b. Necessity comes\nfrom differentiating squared distance along segments from y; sufficiency\ncomes from expanding ||z-alpha-b||^2. Completing the square in phi shows\nthat this projection is its unique minimizing y and its gradient is g.\nThe convexity, differentiability,1-smoothness and unique minimum0 can also\nbe read from accepted factd8b59c7d92425121, applied to this particular K\nand exposing alpha. The exposing condition holds because every nonzero\npoint of K has strictly positive first coordinate. These conclusions do\nnot make the new trajectory estimates accepted facts.\n\n## 3. All actual projection cells, including ties\n\nWrite z=(s,t). The following CLOSED cells cover the plane; overlap on\nboundaries gives the same y,g and objective. The formulas follow by(1).\n\nOrigin vertex:\n\n    u*(s-1)+v*|t|<=0;\n    y=0, g=z, 2phi=||z||^2.                          (2)\n\nInterior of the triangle, with its boundary included:\n\n    1<=s<=1+L, |t|<=(H/L)*(s-1);\n    y=(s-1,t), g=(1,0), 2phi=2s-1.                 (3)\n\nSloping side sigma:\n\n    u<=r_sigma<=u+Rside, w_sigma>=-v;\n    y=(r_sigma-u)*e_sigma,\n    g=u*e_sigma+w_sigma*n_sigma,\n    2phi=2u*r_sigma-u^2+w_sigma^2.                  (4)\n\nIndeed z-alpha has coordinates r_sigma-u,w_sigma+v, because alpha has\ncoordinates u,-v. The first coordinate belongs to the side segment and\nthe second is a nonnegative outward normal. This proves every normal test\nagainst the ENTIRE fixed triangle. The affine height of this proximal\npoint is u*(r_sigma-u); it is not discarded.\n\nVertical base:\n\n    s>=1+L, |t|<=H;\n    y=(L,t), g=(s-L,0), 2phi=2L+(s-L)^2.            (5)\n\nNonzero vertex sigma:\n\n    sigma*t>=H,\n    L*(s-1-L)+H*(sigma*t-H)>=0;\n    y=(L,sigma*H), g=(s-L,t-sigma*H),\n    2phi=2L+(s-L)^2+(t-sigma*H)^2.                 (6)\n\nFor(2), the two tests against(L,+/-H) give its inequality. For(3), the\nprojection residual is zero. For(5), the outward normal is a nonnegative\nmultiple of(1,0). At the vertex in(6), comparison with the other nonzero\nvertex gives sigma*t>=H, and comparison with0 gives the other inequality.\nConversely these tests imply(1). Any projection onto a triangle belongs\nto its interior, an edge, or a vertex, so these cases are exhaustive.\n\nFor every original query and terminal, define y_i=y(z_i), g_i=z_i-y_i,\nf_i=phi(z_i), delta_i=alpha.y_i. Then\n\n    f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,\n    (g_i-alpha).(y_j-y_i)<=0 for ALL i,j.            (7)\n\nAll subsequent examples are actual iterates of this one static function.\nEquation(7) therefore retains every remote original normal automatically,\nrather than selecting compatible-looking faces independently.\n\nFor a physical h=1+d, the side law is exactly\n\n    r_sigma'=r_sigma-h*u, w_sigma'=-d*w_sigma.       (8)\n\nOn the origin cell z'=-d*z; on the interior cell z'=z-h*alpha; on the\nbase s'=L-d*(s-L),t'=t; at vertex b_sigma=(L,sigma*H),\nz'=b_sigma-d*(z-b_sigma). These formulas are used only under their cells.\nThe symmetry axis t=0 is the former scalar segment, but all substantive\nconstructions below have t_0!=0.\n\n## 4. Same-m word estimates, re-proved here\n\nThe length is2^j-1. With A_j=1+S_j, the word gives\nA_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j and A_0=1. Put eta=a/(c-2). Since\nc>1+sqrt(2)>2 and c*(c-2)>1>beta, one has0<eta<1. The solution is\n\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,\n    A_j>=d_j/(c-2), A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    D_j<2c^j for j>=1.                             (9)\n\nThe expression has the right initial value and recurrence, proving it\nby induction. The inequalities use c>2 and0<eta<1. Direct elementary\nbounds, valid for the stated enclosing m, are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2.\n\nFor example7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/1000 imply the bounds on c;\nthose on beta and a follow from their definitions. The product recursion\np_(j+1)=d_j*p_j^2 gives\n\n    p_j=beta^(2^j-1)*c^(-j),\n    d_j*p_j=beta^(2^j)/c.                           (10)\n\nThe signed quadratic product along each nonempty H_j is -p_j, since its\nlength is odd. Every nonempty physical prefix of H_j has unsigned product\nat most a. Induct: a prefix inside the first child uses the child assertion;\na prefix including the bridge has product p_j*d_j times a right-child\nprefix, bounded by1, and p_j*d_j<=a. The base is the sole leaf.\n\nA refinement needed for the switch is that every prefix of length at\nleast2 has product at most a*beta. The base H_1 has no such prefix. In the\ninductive step a prefix in the first child uses induction; a prefix including\nthe bridge has product at most beta^(2^j)/c<=a*beta for j>=1. This also\ncovers the bridge immediately after the first leaf in H_2.\n\nConsequently every nonempty complete child satisfies\n\n    D_j*p_j^2<2c^(-j)<1.                            (11)\n\nAt the enclosing depth,\n\n    D*Q/a^2<18c^(-m)<1,\n    d_m^2/D>c^m/18>4.\n\nThe first uses a>1/3 and(9)-(10). The second uses the same estimates.\nThe final comparisons hold already for m>=7 since c>2. Also S>63 since\nH_m contains more than63 positive entries, each exceeding1. All estimates\nare uniform in the geometry and start; children keep the original m.\n\n## 5. Mechanism1: a coupled identity through a complete side child\n\nFix j>=1 and a sloping side sigma. Suppose every original query of a\ncomplete H_j AND its terminal lies in the closed cell(4) of this side.\nWrite the initial side coordinates r_0,w_0 and the terminal r_end,w_end.\nEquation(8), over the actual child, gives\n\n    r_end=r_0-S_j*u, w_end=-p_j*w_0,\n    2phi(z_end)=2u*r_0-D_j*u^2+p_j^2*w_0^2.\n\nIt follows by expanding one square that\n\n    ||z_0||^2-D_j*2phi(z_end)\n      =(r_0-D_j*u)^2+(1-D_j*p_j^2)*w_0^2>=0.       (12)\n\nThis couples the transported height to the tangential budget and retains\nthe complete normal payment. It is not a separate objective envelope and\nradius envelope. Equations(8) and(12) compose through both complete H_j\nchildren and their bridge whenever the same-side guards hold throughout:\nthe tangential loss is the actual sum2S_j+1+d_j and the signed normal\nfactor is -d_j*p_j^2=-p_(j+1). Thus(12) is an evaluated all-depth invariant\non its guarded domain, with the SAME enclosing parameters.\n\nThe planar bound is sharp: take r_0=D_j*u,w_0=0 and Rside>D_j*u. All\nprefix tangential coordinates decrease from D_j*u to(S_j+1)*u>=u, every\nnormal is0>=-v, and all proximal points stay below the finite cap. For the\nfull word these are off-axis starts for every v>0. Their planar score is\nexactly1/D. Their finite-tilt score is paid in Section7.\n\nFor comparison, an entire interior-cell child has t constant and s reduced\nby S_j; its exact slack is(s_0-D_j)^2+t_0^2. An entire origin-cell child\nhas score p_j^2<1/D_j. A trajectory cannot stay in the same nonzero vertex\ncell across a step h>1: its side-directed residual initially has inner\nproduct at least u>0 with e_sigma, and multiplication by -d makes it\nnegative. Similarly an entire base-cell trajectory cannot persist across\na step: s-L>=1 is sent to -d*(s-L)<0. These observations do not exclude\nreturns to those cells later.\n\n### Why the obvious extension is not proved\n\nThe coordinates in(12) refer to ONE face. On changing sides, both the\nlinear height direction and the quadratic normal coordinate rotate.\nUsing the first child's side identity and then the second child's identity\nwithout paying that rotation changes the original initial-distance term.\nNo inequality justifying that splice has been obtained for arbitrary\nswitches. The next two exact constructions show that the former segment\ncapture arguments cannot supply the missing justification unchanged.\n\n## 6. Two decisive legal failures of scalar tube/capture migration\n\n### 6.1 A complete H_1 violates the interval-distance tube\n\nThe scalar tube would suggest, with Delta(z)=dist(z,K),\n\n    Delta(F_(1+d)(z))<=d*max(Delta(z),1).             (FALSE)\n\nKeep the enclosing m and its actual leaf d=a. Set\n\n    u=sqrt(15)/4, v=1/4, Rside=3,\n    L=3sqrt(15)/4, H=3/4,\n    r_0=u+a/(4sqrt(15)), w_0=2\n\nin upper-side coordinates. The shifted projection is strictly inside the\nupper side: r_0-u=a/(4sqrt(15)) is in(0,3), and w_0+v=9/4>0. Its actual\nleaf output, by(8), has upper coordinates\n\n    r_1=-7a/(2sqrt(15)), w_1=-2a.\n\nThe Euclidean nearest point in K to z_0 is r_0*e_+, since0<r_0<3 and\nw_0>0. Thus Delta(z_0)=2. At z_1 the LOWER coordinates are\n\n    r_1^-=11a/(16sqrt(15)), w_1^-=35a/16.\n\nHere0<r_1^-<3 and w_1^->0, so its Euclidean nearest point in K is\nr_1^-*e_- and Delta(z_1)=35a/16>2a. The claimed tube fails strictly.\n\nFor the objective projection at the terminal, r_1^+<u and r_1^-<u,\nso both vertex0 inequalities hold and y(z_1)=0. Thus the initial query is\non the genuine upper-side cell, the actual complete leaf ends in the\norigin cell, and both original guards are explicit. There is no freely\nchosen output face. In global coordinates the off-axis initial point is\n\n    z_0=((7+a)/16,(135+a)/(16sqrt(15))).\n\nAll parameters are finite. Section7 embeds this very same child in three\ndimensions with any finite T, without altering its planar dynamics. The\nfailure concerns the proposed distance tube, not a worst-case objective\nbound. In particular retaining only interval distance loses an angular\ncomponent even in a complete original child.\n\n### 6.2 A whole H_m defeats the small-radius capture, at finite tilt\n\nChoose the geometry AND start explicitly as follows:\n\n    u=d_m/(2D), v=sqrt(1-u^2),\n    Rside=d_m, L=u*d_m, H=v*d_m,\n    r_0=d_m/2=D*u, w_0=0, z_0=r_0*e_+.\n\nThese are legitimate positive finite triangle parameters. Indeed(9) gives\nS>=d_m/(c-2)-1>2d_m-1, hence D>4d_m-1>d_m, since d_m>1.\nThus0<u<1. Every original query and terminal stays on the upper side:\n\n    r_i=u*(D-U_i), w_i=0,\n    U_i=sum_(0<=k<i)h_k, 0<=U_i<=S.\n\nTherefore u<=r_i<=d_m/2<u+Rside, w_i>=-v; the terminal has\nr_n=u*(S+1)>u. Formula(12) gives\n\n    2phi(z_n)=D*u^2=d_m^2/(4D)>1.                  (13)\n\nThe scalar complete-child capture would have put terminal radius below\na<1 and terminal2phi below1 for inputs of radius at most d_m. Both\nconclusions fail here. This is an actual entire word, not an incompatible\nface prescription.\n\nThere is also a finite orthogonal original instance. Set T=1 and take\nX_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0), where(s_0,t_0)=z_0. Then\n\n    ||X_0||^2=r_0^2*(1+u^2)<d_m^2,\n    2Phi(X_n)=d_m^2/(4D)+Q*u^2*r_0^2>1.\n\nIts normalized score is exactly\n\n    E=(1/D+u^2*Q)/(1+u^2),\n    1/(2D)<E<1/D, and E>1/d_m^2.                 (14)\n\nThe first lower bound uses u<1; the last uses d_m^2/D>4. The strict\nupper bound uses Q<1/D. Thus the true finite-radius version of the old\nsmall-start score bound also fails, while the requested17/(16D) threshold\nis NOT exceeded. Its role is solely to kill that intermediate invariant.\n\nThe obstruction is precise: an off-axis ray can have height slope u much\nsmaller than||alpha||=1. Its tangential drift is h*u, not h. The coupled\nside identity retains this u; a radius capture independent of angle loses\nit. Neither construction rejects the full triangle research direction.\n\n## 7. The exact finite-tilt embedding and true denominator\n\nEmbed the one triangle in the plane with third coordinate0 and put\n\n    Ktilde={(y_1,y_2,0):(y_1,y_2) in K},\n    lambda=(1,0,T), T>0,\n    Phi(X)=min_(Y in Ktilde)[lambda.Y+||X-Y||^2/2].\n\nFor X=(z,q), the definition separates exactly:\n\n    Phi(z,q)=phi(z)+q^2/2, grad Phi=(g(z),q).\n\nThe height remains y_1, so0 is still the unique minimum of the full\nfunction. Choose the required orthogonal initial point\n\n    X_0=(s,t,-s/T), lambda.X_0=0.\n\nThen the planar trajectory is exactly the one already derived, and\nq_n=(-p_m)*q_0. The TRUE squared initial distance to the complete minimum\nset{0} and the exact terminal score are\n\n    Rtrue^2=s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2,\n    E_T=[2phi(z_n)+Q*s^2/T^2]/[s^2+t^2+s^2/T^2].     (15)\n\nFor z_0!=0, define E_plane=2phi(z_n)/(s^2+t^2) and\nchi=s^2/[T^2*(s^2+t^2)]. Then\n\n    E_T=(E_plane+chi*Q)/(1+chi).                    (16)\n\nThis is an exact convex combination, not a free height correction. Any\nplanar upper at least Q passes to finite tilt. If E_plane=1/D and s!=0,\nevery finite T has E_T<1/D. The supremum1/D in the fixed-side subfamily is\napproached as finite T grows, not attained by declaring T=infinity.\nThe instances in Sections6 and10 choose an actual finite T. A hypothetical\nstrict planar witness above a target q_target>Q would require\nT^2>s^2*(q_target-Q)/[(s^2+t^2)*(E_plane-q_target)] for its finite lift to\nremain strict; no such witness is claimed here.\n\n## 8. Mechanism2: exactly one first-leaf upper-to-lower switch pattern\n\nThis mechanism starts only after the decisive failures in Section6. It\nopens just the following pattern, for the ORIGINAL H_m:\n\n    original query0: upper sloping-side cell;\n    original samples1,2,...,n: lower sloping-side cell.\n\nThe terminal condition is part of the pattern. Boundary ties are allowed\nin the general bound; Section10 supplies a member with strict side guards.\nThe entire remainder of H_m stays present. This does not authorize a\nsecond pattern at the root or independent face choices within a child.\n\nLet(r,w) be the lower coordinates of the actual state z_1 after the first\nleaf ell. Write\n\n    M=S-ell, Drem=1+2M=D-2ell,\n    kappa=u^2-v^2, omega=2u*v.\n\nThe change of coordinates is\n\n    e_-.e_+=kappa, e_-.n_+=-omega,\n    n_-.e_+=-omega, n_-.n_+=-kappa.\n\nInverting just the first physical leaf via(8) gives its UNIQUE possible\ninitial upper coordinates:\n\n    r_0=ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w,\n    w_0=(omega*r+kappa*w)/a.                        (17)\n\nConsequently the original planar squared radius is exactly\n\n    N0=(ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w)^2\n                          +(omega*r+kappa*w)^2/a^2. (18)\n\nThis initial denominator is never replaced by r^2+w^2 at the switch.\n\nFor original time i>=1, let V_i=sum_(1<=k<i)h_k and let\nb_i=product_(1<=k<i)(1-h_k), with V_1=0,b_1=1. Then the proposed lower\ncoordinates are\n\n    r_i=r-u*V_i, w_i=b_i*w.                         (19)\n\nThese are actual iff every corresponding lower cell guard holds, along\nwith the initial upper cell in(17). We now eliminate the entire prefix\nnormal guard list analytically.\n\n## 9. All original guards, chronological payment, and uniform strict bound\n\n### 9.1 Exact guard reduction for this pattern\n\nTangential lower coordinates decrease monotonically with original mass.\nTherefore every tangential guard in samples1,...,n is equivalent to\n\n    u*(M+1)=u*(S-a)<=r<=u+Rside.                    (20)\n\nBy the refined prefix product in Section4, |b_i|<=beta for every i>=2.\nThe first of these is b_2=-beta, since the second physical entry of H_m\nis1+d_1=1+beta. At i=1, b_1=1. Consequently ALL the lower normal guards\nare equivalent to\n\n    -v<=w<=v/beta.                                 (21)\n\nNecessity uses i=1 and i=2. For sufficiency, if w>=0, negative b_i have\nmagnitude at most beta and positive ones cause no violation; if w<=0,\npositive b_i have magnitude at most1, and negative ones cause no violation.\nThis includes the terminal, whose signed suffix factor is p_m/a>0.\n\nThe remaining exact guards are simply the initial upper ones:\n\n    u<=ell*u+kappa*r-omega*w<=u+Rside,\n    (omega*r+kappa*w)/a>=-v.                        (22)\n\nEquations(20)-(22) are necessary AND sufficient: if they hold, (17)\nprojects to the upper side, its first update lands exactly at(r,w) in the\nlower frame, and induction with(19) and the proved guard reduction keeps\nevery subsequent sample in the lower cell of the SAME finite triangle.\nNo remote normal or initial cost is being relaxed.\n\n### 9.2 Geometry enforces a lower bound on the angle cosine\n\nEvery legal pattern satisfies u^2>1/4. To prove it, the left inequality in\n(22) gives\n\n    kappa*r>=omega*w-a*u>=-(2v^2+a)*u.\n\nIf kappa<=-1/2, it follows that\nr<=2*(2v^2+a)*u<5u. But(20) and S>63 imply\nr>=u*(S-a)>5u, a contradiction. Thus kappa>-1/2 and u^2=(1+kappa)/2>1/4.\nPut k=u*r. It then follows that\n\n    k> (S-a)/4>15.                                 (23)\n\nThis rules out a vanishing tangential height direction in THIS long\nsingle-switch pattern by paying the full remaining physical mass. It is\nnot an angle restriction imposed on the original triangle family.\n\n### 9.3 The original radius pays the rotated height and normal\n\nDefine the exact excess over the unrestarted comparison radius by\n\n    Delta=N0-(r+ell*u)^2-w^2.\n\nSince the rotation preserves r^2+w^2, expansion of(18) gives\n\n    Delta=(a^(-2)-1)*(omega*r+kappa*w)^2\n                    -2ell*u*((1-kappa)*r+omega*w). (24)\n\nWe prove Delta>0 on the WHOLE guarded domain. By(21), |w|<=v/beta.\nSince |kappa|<1, omega=2u*v, beta>99/100 and(23),\n\n    omega*r+kappa*w >= v*(2k-1/beta)>v*k>0.\n\nAlso1-kappa=2v^2, and hence\n\n    2ell*u*((1-kappa)*r+omega*w)\n       <=4ell*v^2*(k+u^2/beta)\n       <6v^2*(k+2).\n\nUsing a<1/2 in(24) therefore gives\n\n    Delta>v^2*(3k^2-6k-12)>0.                      (25)\n\nFor the last strict inequality, k>15>4 and the polynomial is positive\nat4 and increasing for k>=4. All these are exact symbolic comparisons.\nIn particular this coupling is UNIFORM in all legal u,v,r,w,Rside; it\nhas no hidden geometry-dependent threshold or constant.\n\n### 9.4 Full terminal objective and evaluated payment identity\n\nThe entire original suffix, of even length n-1, has signed normal factor\np_m/a. Equations(4),(19) give the ACTUAL terminal objective\n\n    E_num=2phi(z_n)=2u*r-Drem*u^2+(Q/a^2)*w^2.       (26)\n\nCombine(24) with one square expansion:\n\n    N0-D*E_num\n       =Delta+[r-(D-ell)*u]^2+[1-D*Q/a^2]*w^2>0.   (27)\n\nThe square identity uses Drem=D-2ell exactly. Its normal coefficient is\nstrictly positive by Section4, and Delta>0 by(25). Thus\n\n    E_plane=E_num/N0<1/D                            (28)\n\nfor EVERY legal member of this pattern at EVERY enclosing m>=1536.\nSince Q<1/D, the exact finite-tilt formula(16) also gives E_T<1/D for\nevery finite T>0. No all-start triangle statement is inferred from this.\n\nThis is the chronological counterpart of the segment exterior pairing for\nthis one permitted switch: the first leaf's inverse amplifies the original\nnormal by1/a, the rotation mixes it into the future height direction, and\n(24)-(27) pay both with the original initial radius. Omitting that inverse\ncost would erase Delta and could produce an incorrect gain. The proof does\nnot use scalar sign ordering or assume a positive-to-positive persistence\nproperty in the plane.\n\n## 10. An explicit finite legal full-word switch family\n\nThe domain just bounded is nonempty at every m>=1536. Fix once and for all\n\n    u=sqrt(3)/2, v=1/2, kappa=1/2, omega=sqrt(3)/2,\n    r=(D-ell)*u, w=0,\n    r_0=ell*u+kappa*r, w_0=omega*r/a,\n    Rside=r+r_0+1, L=u*Rside, H=v*Rside,\n    z_0=r_0*e_++w_0*n_+.\n\nThese are finite explicit parameters, with no scan or parameter optimization.\nThe initial upper tangential projection is r_0-u=a*u+kappa*r>0 and is\nstrictly less than Rside. For every later original sample,\n\n    z_i=[r-u*V_i]*e_-, 1<=i<=n.\n\nThe smallest tangential coordinate is at the terminal:\n\n    r-u*M=u*(S+1)>u.\n\nThe largest is r<u+Rside. (29)\n\nFor a fully concrete orthogonal instance choose T=1 and\nX_0=(s_0,t_0,-s_0), with\ns_0=u*r_0-v*w_0 and t_0=v*r_0+u*w_0. Both actual H2 certificates are retained.\nThe new ingredient is the ACTUAL auxiliary projection at the input of the\nright H2, with its actual trailing mass. A primitive-left/bridge identity\nhas a positive explicit coefficient, bounded below by3C(C+k)/46 in this\nH3 application, where C=1+S2 and k=a*c^2. This pays the old negative\nprimitive term without assuming primitive positivity of H3.\n\nA natural attempt to propagate the same join rule with only U laws for\nboth children is FALSE. Two children consisting of the single step3/2\nboth satisfy the universal U law for all d>=1. Their join with bridge9/4\npasses the new scalar bridge condition, yet on the ONE fixed interval\nD0=[-1,1] it violates U for every1<=d<175/154. At d=1 its ratio is575/512.\nThis is a legal counterexample to that concrete reusable U-only join,\nnot to the actual perturbed H4 or to every possible richer invariant.\n\nThe positive reusable law still requires a primitive left reserve. It\ntherefore closes H3 but does not propagate through arbitrary higher\nsymmetric depths, whose left children need not be primitive. No uniform\nall-depth loss1+C/m or full original-function-class bound is obtained.\nThe original unrestricted minimax problem remains OPEN. No fact_id was\ncreated. Exactly two substantive mechanisms were used.\n\n## 1. Fixed parameters, bodies, and accepted scope\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536, throughout all children. Define\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, C_j=1+S_j.\n\nThe physical H2 is (A,B,A), where A=1+a and B=1+beta. Write\n\n    s=S2=2A+B, C=1+s, k=a*c^2=beta*c,\n    h=C+k, S=S3=s+(1+k)+s=2C+k-1.\n\nThus h is the MASS of the prefix H2 followed by its root bridge, not an\nextra physical step. The seven physical steps are\n(A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A). Neither m nor the leaf parameters reset.\n\nFor one fixed nonempty compact convex set D0 in a finite-dimensional real\nEuclidean space, containing0, put\n\n    P(v)=argmin_(g in D0)||v-g||^2,\n    F(v)=max_(g in D0)(<v,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    U_d(v)=max_(g in D0)(2<v,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.\n\nA physical word uses x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i and g_i=P(x_i).\nAll auxiliary projections in this report also use this SAME D0.\nNo approximation to the physical trajectory is used.\n\nThe accepted interpolation orientation is fact1252b8823bb5a290:\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.       (1.1)\n\nIts necessity proof was reread. The complete applicable primitive and\ndominant composition definitions in accepted04219554e41d9750, Sections\n10-11, were reread. Its exact-root join is not invoked at an off-root\nbridge. The identities needed here, including the old H2 draft, are\nproved jointly below. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a FULL-class\nupper before its conditional negative conclusion applies; no such upper\nis inferred here. This is the gradient-projection diagnostic, not the\nresidual-gradient affine-height representation. W=2R remains unchanged.\n\nFor clarity, the scalar s here is S2; the constant sqrt(2)-1 is denoted u.\nThe original problem is the asymptotic unrestricted comparison of W_n^star\nwith the original ALL-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not this finite H3 law.\n\n## 2. Elementary projection facts and exact original rows\n\nProjection optimality is\n\n    <v-P(v),g-P(v)> <=0 for all g in D0.                 (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by minimizing squared distance on each segment from\nP(v) to g. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||v-g||^2. The minimizer is\nunique by strict convexity of squared distance. Adding the two conditions\nfor inputs v,w gives ||P(v)-P(w)||^2<=<v-w,P(v)-P(w)>; hence P is\n1-Lipschitz. Completion of the square identifies P(v) as the unique\nmaximizer in F. The two maximizing inequalities bound the first-order\nremainder of F by ||t||||P(v+t)-P(v)||, proving grad F=P. Thus F is\nconvex, differentiable and1-smooth.\n\nIts minimum is0. The minimizers are exactly\n\n    K0={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in D0}.\n\nIf z is in K0, all candidates in F(z) are nonpositive and g=0 attains0.\nIf some <z,g> is positive, sufficiently small positive multiples of g\nbelong to D0 and give a positive value. For every z_star in K0 and M>0,\n\n    U_M(x)<=||x-z_star||^2/M,                           (2.2)\n\nby completing the square in2<x-z_star,g>-M||g||^2 and using\n<z_star,g><=0. No origin-radius replacement is made.\n\nAt physical or auxiliary samples of this same F, direct substitution of\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 into (1.1) gives\n\n    Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j>.                            (2.3)\n\nEvery row below is therefore an actual same-body projection normal.\nAuxiliary samples introduce no physical gradient-descent step.\n\nFor a word b of mass t, input v, endpoint z, define its primitive energy\n\n    V_b=2sum_(i<N)b_i(f_i-f_N)\n        +sum_(i<N)b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2-t(t+1)||g_N||^2.\n\nTelescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 proves\n\n    V_b=||v||^2-||z+tP(z)||^2.                         (2.4)\n\nIndeed the value and nonterminal gradient terms sum to\n||v||^2-||z||^2-2t f_N; substitution of f_N gives (2.4).\n\n## 3. H2 and off-root identities retained jointly\n\nFor an actual step x_v=x_u-l g_u, the two ordered rows are\n\n Q_uv=f_u-f_v+(1/2-l)||g_u||^2-<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2,\n Q_vu=f_v-f_u+||g_u||^2/2+(l-1)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2/2.\n\nCoefficient collection gives\n\n V_(l)=-2Q_uv-2(1+l)Q_vu+(2-l^2)||g_u-g_v||^2,          (3.1)\n ||g_u||^2-||g_v||^2\n   =(2/l-1)||g_u-g_v||^2-(2/l)(Q_uv+Q_vu), l>0.         (3.2)\n\nThese identities keep both formal gradients.\n\nFor general left and right words with capacities P_L and P_R, insert the\nactual bridge1+t, 0<=t<P_R. Let p be its query and z the parent endpoint;\nj ranges over the nonterminal right queries with steps b_j. Define\n\n K=(2P_L+P_R+t)/(P_R-t),\n Delta=P_L P_R-t(P_L+P_R+t),\n R=(P_L+t)Delta/(P_R-t),\n E=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+t Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).\n\nOn the actual history, without any primitive assumption, one has\n\n    V_parent=V_left+K V_right-(K-1)E\n                         +R(||g_p||^2-||g_z||^2).       (3.3)\n\nHere is a coefficient check of the off-root term. Expansion of E gives\nvalue coefficients\n\n    2sum_j b_j f_j+(t-P_R)f_p+(2-P_R-t)f_z\n\nand gradient terms\n\n sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2\n +[(P_R-t)/2-t^2]||g_p||^2+(P_R+t)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nAll mixed terms cancel: with G=sum b_j g_j, the two sums contribute\nrespectively t<G,g_p>+||G||^2/2 and-||G||^2/2-<G,g_z>, in addition to\nhalf their displayed diagonals; Q_(z,p) contributes t<g_z,g_p>+<G,g_z>,\nand tQ_(p,z) contributes-t<g_p,G>-t<g_p,g_z>. The remaining value\ncoefficients match because(K-1)(P_R-t)=2(P_L+t). The unmatched gradient\ncoefficients are P_L^2-Kt^2=R and K P_R^2-(P_L+P_R+t)^2=-R. Factoring\nafter multiplication by P_R-t verifies these two identities. This proves\n(3.3) without an exact-root substitution or a changed trajectory.\n\nFor H2 only, put C1=1+A=2+a and\n\n K1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),\n Delta1=C1^2-beta(2C1+beta), R1=(C1+beta)Delta1/(C1-beta),\n deltaA=2-A^2.\n\nLet u=sqrt(2)-1. (3.6)\n\nAll other multipliers are zero; none is negative. The value coefficients\nare(2A,2B,2A,-2s): for the bridge they give\n-2A+(K1-1)(C1-beta)=2B, and at the last nonterminal point they give\n-2(K1-1)A+2K1 A=2A. The square remainder is a positive weighted path\nLaplacian on all four gradients. No formal coordinates are identified.\n\nThere is also the conservative bound\n\n    e_i>=epsilon^2/2, i=0,1,2.                       (3.7)\n\nTo check it, deltaA=[2u epsilon/(1+epsilon)](sqrt(2)+A)>epsilon/2;\nu>1/4, epsilon<1, and sqrt(2)+A>2 suffice. Formula (3.4), u<1/2,\nand C1>2 give uC1-beta>epsilon/4 and lambda C1+beta>4. Hence\nDelta1>epsilon and R1>epsilon. Since B<2, (3.7) follows. These facts\nare derived here, not assumed from the earlier unverified H2 report.\n\nFor the actual H3 root use C=1+s and k=a*c^2. Thus Delta2=(uC-k)(lambda C+k)<0. Primitive positivity is\nnot a premise of the H3 proof.\n\n## 4. Every actual maximization remainder\n\nFor any actual word of mass t, input v, endpoint z and trailing d>=1,\nwrite\n\n M=d+2t, q=P(z/d), p=P(v/M),\n bbar=[v-z+(d+t)q]/M,\n Rem(v,M,bbar)=M||p-bbar||^2+2<v-Mp,p-bbar>.           (4.1)\n\nThe point bbar belongs to D0: its physical gradient weights are the\nactual nonnegative steps, and its q weight is d+t; their sum is M.\nEach term in Rem is nonnegative by (2.1). Comparing the maximum at p\nwith the feasible point bbar, then expanding the squares, gives EXACTLY\n\n U_M(v)-U_d(z)\n   =[||v||^2-||z+tq||^2]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).             (4.2)\n\nFor instance v-M bbar=z-(d+t)q, which makes the expansion immediate:\nthe numerator after subtracting M U_d(z) is\n||v||^2-||z||^2-2t<z,q>-t^2||q||^2.\n\nPut g=P(z). Comparing the optimizers of U_d(z) at two different d values\ngives, for1<=d1<d2 and q_i=P(z/d_i),\n\n d1(||q1||^2-||q2||^2)\n <=2<z,q1-q2><=d2(||q1||^2-||q2||^2).\n\nIt follows that the squared-norm and inner-product differences are\nnonnegative. Consequently the exact radial term\n\n Lrad(t,z,d)=2t<z,g-q>+t^2(||g||^2-||q||^2)>=0         (4.3)\n\nsatisfies\n\n U_M(v)-U_d(z)=[V_word+Lrad(t,z,d)]/M+Rem(v,M,bbar).  (4.4)\n\nThis proves the H2 U law from (3.5), for ALL starts and d>=1. It also\nretains the actual remainders, instead of assigning them values from a\ndifferent trajectory.\n\nAll remainders are themselves sums of original augmented normal rows\nand one square. In (4.1), let bbar=sum theta_i g_i with theta_i>=0 and\nsum theta_i=1, including the actual auxiliary q sample. The initial\nauxiliary sample has input v/M and gradient p. Formula (2.3) gives\n\n Rem=M||p-bbar||^2-2M sum_i theta_i Q_(i,p).           (4.5)\n\nHere Q_(i,p)=<v/M-p,g_i-p>. Thus no separate unproved positivity\nprinciple is concealed in the notation Rem.\n\n## 5. Mechanism1: the primitive-left / actual-auxiliary bridge identity\n\nThis is the new payment. Keep a GENERAL left word of mass s_L and\ncapacity C_L=1+s_L, with actual input x and endpoint v. Let a_p=P(v).\nApply the ACTUAL bridge1+k, k>=0, to obtain\n\n w=v-(1+k)a_p, h=C_L+k=s_L+1+k.\n\nLet D>0 be an independent trailing parameter, and define\n\n M=D+2h, r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),\n zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,\n Np=<v-a_p,a_p-r>, Nr=<w-D r,r-a_p>,\n Theta=h[C_L D-k(C_L+D+k)]/(D+k).                  (5.1)\n\nBoth Np and Nr are nonnegative. In augmented row notation,\nNp=-Q_(r,p), Nr=-D Q_(p,r), where the r sample has input w/D and the\np sample is the physical bridge query v. Every point is actual.\nThe mixture zP uses all left physical gradients, the bridge gradient\nwith weight1+k, and r with weightD+h. These weights sum to M.\n\nWithout any assumed signs for the primitive energy V_L, one has\n\n U_M(x)-U_D(w)\n   = [V_L+Theta||a_p-r||^2\n        +2h(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+2h C_L/(D+k)Nr]/M\n       +Rem(x,M,zP).                                (5.2)\n\nHere is a full derivation. Put X=v+s_L a_p and e=a_p-r. Then\n\n w+h r=X-h e,\n ||x||^2-||w+h r||^2=V_L+2h<X,e>-h^2||e||^2.        (5.3)\n\nThe actual projection normals give the exact relations\n\n Np+Nr=(D+k)<a_p,e>-D||e||^2,\n <X,e>=C_L<a_p,e>+Np\n       =C_L D||e||^2/(D+k)\n          +(1+C_L/(D+k))Np+C_L Nr/(D+k).            (5.4)\n\nSubstitute (5.4) in (5.3) and then apply (4.2) to the prefix mass h\nwith trailing D. The square coefficient is\nh[2C_L D/(D+k)-h]=Theta, proving (5.2).\n\nTherefore, if\n\n    V_L>=0 and C_L D>=k(C_L+D+k),                   (5.5)\n\nthen U_D(w)<=U_(D+2h)(x). This is a reusable quantified lemma with\nindependent C_L,D and actual physical bridge k. The numerical capacity\ncondition is not a substitute for the displayed actual left reserve.\nA right word of mass s_R may be appended if its actual U law holds at\ntrailing d; use D=d+2s_R. No equality C_L=1+s_R is required by (5.2).\n\n## 6. Application to both actual H2 children: nonnegative H3 identity\n\nNow specialize to the actual H3. Write\n\n x=x0, v=x3, a_p=g3, w=x4, y=x7, b=g7,\n D=d+2s, M=d+2S=D+2h,\n q=P(y/d), r=P(w/D), p0=P(x/M),\n zR=[w-y+(d+s)q]/D,\n zP=[x-w+(D+h)r]/M,\n RR=Rem(w,D,zR), RP=Rem(x,M,zP),\n LR=Lrad(s,y,d).\n\nThe initial maximizer of the right child is EXACTLY r, the final\nauxiliary maximizer in (5.2). There is no independent choice or reset.\nLet V_L and V_R be the actual H2 energies (3.5), using respectively\nindices0,1,2,3 and4,5,6,7.\n\nThe scalar coefficient is strictly positive for every d>=1. Since\nbeta>9/10, c<5/2, one has a>1/3, C=4+2a+beta>5, and\nk=lambda(1-epsilon^2)<5/2<C/2. Also D>=2C-1. Hence\n\n C D-k(C+D+k)=(C-k)D-k(C+k)\n   > (C/2)(2C-1)-3C^2/4=C(C-2)/4>0.                (6.1)\n\nThere is a uniform-in-d lower bound for the actual square coefficient:\nD>9C/5 and k<C/2 imply k/(D+k)<5/23, so\n\n Theta/h=C-k-2Ck/(D+k)>C/2-10C/23=3C/46,\n Theta>3C(C+k)/46.                                 (6.2)\n\nSumming (5.2) and the actual right-child identity (4.4) gives\n\n U_M(x)-U_d(y)\n  = V_L/M+(V_R+LR)/D\n    +[Theta||a_p-r||^2\n       +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2h C/(D+k)Nr]/M\n    +RP+RR.                                        (6.3)\n\nEvery term on the right is nonnegative after substituting the complete\nH2 formula (3.5). Equations (3.5)-(3.6), (4.5), and the explicit two\nnormal rows after (5.1) make (6.3) a nonnegative original-row identity\non the physical and actual auxiliary samples of one fixed body.\nIn particular it retains the actual adjacent-gradient reserves\n\n epsilon^2/(2M) sum_(i=0)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2\n +epsilon^2/(2D) sum_(i=4)^6||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2,\n\nas well as the root auxiliary square of coefficientTheta/M.\nNo claim is made that the adjacent squares alone pay the root defect.\n\nThis proves the stated all-body H3 inequality WITHIN THIS JOINT DRAFT.\nAt d=1, U1=2F; (2.2) also yields\n\n    2F(T_H3 x)<=dist(x,K0)^2/(1+2S3).\n\nThis remains a projection-gradient-class assertion. Exact reconciliation with the negative R2 term\n\nTo show explicitly that the old off-root term has been paid, retain\nalso the full-parent objects demanded by the assignment:\n\n zF=[x-y+(d+S)q]/M,\n RF=Rem(x,M,zF), LF=Lrad(S,y,d).\n\nThese are generally DIFFERENT from zP and RP. Formula (4.4), followed\nby (3.8), gives the same parent gap as\n\n M[U_M(x)-U_d(y)]\n  =V_L+K2 V_R-(K2-1)E2\n        +R2(||a_p||^2-||b||^2)+LF+M RF.             (7.1)\n\nMultiplying (6.3) by M gives its second, nonnegative representation:\n\n V_L+(M/D)(V_R+LR)+Theta||a_p-r||^2\n   +2h(1+C/(D+k))Np+2h C/(D+k)Nr+M(RP+RR). Mandatory fixed-interval cap/cap check\n\nUse the ONE interval D0=[-k/C,1], put rcap=k/C, and start x=C.\nEvery left H2 prefix of mass t in[0,C-1] has point C-t>=1 and gradient1.\nThe endpoint is v=1. Every\nE2 row is zero: the physical normal at v=1 is zero, the terminal normal\nat y=-rcap is zero, and all right gradients agree. Nonetheless\n\n V_H3=C^2-(2C+k)^2 rcap^2=R2(1-rcap^2)<0.           (8.1)\n\nThus (6.3) has not inadvertently reinstated primitive positivity.\nFor every d>=1 all the relevant maximizers are interior or boundary\npoints of this SAME interval, with explicit values\n\n D=d+2(C-1), M=d+2S,\n p0=C/M, q=-rcap/d, r=-k/D.\n\nThe full-parent mixture and remainder are\n\n zF=(C-S rcap/d)/M,\n x-Mp0=0, RF=S^2 rcap^2/(d^2 M). This diagnostic agrees with the universal candidate, but\nits remainder formulas are used ONLY for this history.\n\n## 9. Mechanism2: a concrete reusable U-only join is false\n\nAfter the H3 outcome, test the following natural extension of (5.5):\n\n Proposed U-only join. Suppose each child word has\n U_d(T_child z)<=U_(d+2mass(child))(z) for every fixed compact convex\n body containing0, every start and every d>=1. Set C_L=1+s_L and\n D=d+2s_R. If C_L D>=k(C_L+D+k), then their physical join\n (left,1+k,right) has the same U law at its actual total mass.\n\nThis statement keeps d and the two masses independent. Its difference\nfrom (5.5) is precisely replacement of the actual left primitive reserve\nby a U-only hypothesis. The following family disproves it.\n\nFirst derive the needed universal child law jointly. For one physical\nstep l=3/2, write a=P(x), y=x-l a, q=P(y/d), M=d+2l=d+3. Every ingredient used below is proved within it.\nNo earlier unverified finding is a correctness premise.\n\n**Outcome.** For every positive first inner reversing level J, there is a\ncomplete full-word objective bound strictly below 4/(5D) for every start\nwhose FIRST complete H_J ends in the closed core. The second, capacity-amplitude\nmechanism is not activated in this allocation.\n\n## 1. Fixed data and the precise scoped result\n\nFix ONE integer m>=1536. Every child uses these SAME parameters:\n\n    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\n    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),\n    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,\n    D=1+2S_m, n=2^m-1,\n    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.\n\nHere h_i denotes a chronological physical entry of H_m when no level\nsuperscript is present. d_m is auxiliary, not an extra physical step.\n\nFix 0<kappa<=1 and finite B>=kappa, and keep the ONE function\n\n    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,\n    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,\n    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,\n    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r), r>=0.\n\nLet Psi_j(s) be the actual output of the complete word H_j on this\nfixed function from s. Its exact interval radius is\n\n    M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|, R>=0.\n\nFor the theorem below assume that\n\n    J=min{j in {0,...,m-1}: kappa*(1+d_j)>1}\n    exists and 1<=J<=m-1.                              (1.1)\n\nThus every physical core step in H_J is nonreversing. Define\n\n    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)=1+1/(c-2),\n    t=kappa*d_J, q=kappa*(1+d_J)-1=t-v,\n    L_J=S_J/d_J, N=m-J-1,\n    K_fr(z)=z for 0<=z<=1, K_fr(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1,\n    Z_fr=K_fr(q)*exp(-L_J*t).                                (1.2)\n\nK_fr is only a scalar comparison function, not a new gradient law.\n\nFor a nonzero actual initial point s put R=|s| and\n\n    y=|Psi_J(s)|.\n\n**Scoped full-word theorem (joint unverified).** If y<=1, then the actual\ntrajectory under the complete H_m obeys\n\n    2f(Psi_m(s))/R^2 < 4/(5D).                          (1.3)\n\nIf the initial H_J contains no strict sign crossing, the stronger bound\n1/(2D) holds. A zero hit gives zero payoff.\n\nAll 0<R<=v Q satisfy y<=1, hence satisfy (1.3), uniformly in B, kappa,\nm and the positive level J. The initial H_J may have any number of\nexterior crossings before it enters the core. It may enter the core\non its actual last leaf, including by a same-side middle-to-core step.\n\nFor the complete first block H_(J+1), let r_(J+1) be its terminal radius.\nThe proof supplies the actual-energy state\n\n    r_(J+1)<=y Z_fr,    Z_fr<1/4,\n    kappa*d_(J+1)*r_(J+1)<25/32,\n    G(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1),\n    2F(r_(J+1))=kappa*r_(J+1)^2.                       (1.4)\n\nThe first reversing bridge may have landed outside radius1. That\nexcursion is proved to return during its following COMPLETE H_J.\nIt is not treated as a quadratic trajectory.\n\nFor each later complete initial block H_l, J+1<=l<=m, its actual\nterminal radius r_l remains in the core and satisfies\n\n    kappa*d_l*r_l\n      <(25/32)*beta^(2(l-J-1)),\n    r_l<=y Z_fr*theta^(l-J-1),\n    G(r_l)=kappa*r_l, 2F(r_l)=kappa*r_l^2.            (1.5)\n\nEvery intervening physical prefix also remains in the core after\ncompletion of that first H_(J+1). The exact final suffix formula is\n\n    |Psi_m(s)|\n      =r_(J+1)*product_(l=J+1)^(m-1)(q_l*P_l),\n    q_l=kappa*(1+d_l)-1,\n    P_l=product_(h in H_l)|1-kappa*h|.                 (1.6)\n\nAn empty product is1. All factors and every final physical leaf are\npresent. These are actual objective and gradient values, not merely\nan absolute gradient cap.\n\nThe two excluded cases J=0 and no physical inner reversal are not\nclaimed as new domains here. Prior drafts cover specified portions of\nthem; those prior statements are not needed below.\n\n## 2. Legality, the true energy, and ordered interpolation rows\n\nDirect integration gives\n\n    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                          0<=r<=1,\n    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                    1<=r<=u,\n    2F(r)=2B*r-[B^2+v*(2B-kappa)],            r>=u.    (2.1)\n\nAt r=1 the first two values equal kappa. At r=u the latter two\nvalues equal B^2+kappa*v. Their derivatives also agree at the knots.\nB=kappa collapses the middle interval; kappa=1 makes v=0.\n\nThe derivative g is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes kappa,\n1 and0 on its pieces. Partitioning an arbitrary interval at its knots\nproves |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Thus f is finite, convex, differentiable,\nand globally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0,\nand the true initial distance is R. The objective normalization is W=2R\nin the project's notation: our payoff is 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2.\n\nFor x<y let Delta=g(y)-g(x). Then 0<=Delta<=y-x and\n\n    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),\n    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)),  x<=t<=y.\n\nIntegration proves both directed smooth-convex remainders are at least\nDelta^2/2. Hence for EVERY pair of actual physical, terminal, or optimum\nsamples (x_i,g_i,f_i),\n\n    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.          (2.2)\n\nRepeated positions have identical values and gradients. The full scalar\nGram matrix is positive semidefinite because each of its quadratic\nforms is a square of a real linear combination of the actual vectors.\nNo formal terminal gradient or interpolation pair is removed.\n\nFor advertised smoothness L_adv>0, the objective L_adv*f with steps\nh_i/L_adv has the same trajectory and normalized payoff.\n\nWe use the elementary lower bound\n\n    G(r)>=kappa*min(r,1), r>=0.                       (2.3)\n\nInside the core it is equality. Outside the core, both B and\nmax(kappa*r,r-v) are at least kappa. This lower bound does not replace\nthe actual law, its cap, or its kink.\n\n## 3. Exact interval map and branch ties\n\nFor a physical h>=1 put d=h-1 and q_h=|1-h*kappa|. The exact interval\nimage radius is\n\n    E_h(R)=max{\n      q_h*min(R,1),\n      [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,\n      [R-h*B]_+\n    },                                               (3.1)\n\nwhere [z]_+=max(z,0).\n\nFor a positive input radius r the signed update is\n\n    (1-h*kappa)*r,                 0<=r<=1,\n    h*v-d*r,                      1<=r<=u,\n    r-h*B,                        r>=u.\n\nAt r=1 the two expressions agree because h*v-d=1-h*kappa.\nAt r=u they agree because h*v-d*u=v-d*B=u-h*B.\nThus the knot ties, and the collapsed middle interval at B=kappa,\nare retained.\n\nOn each available affine piece the maximum absolute value occurs at an\nendpoint. The core contributes the first term. On an available middle\npiece the positive endpoint is bounded by the core endpoint, and the\nnegative endpoint contributes the second term. On an available tail\npiece, its negative endpoint is the middle endpoint, and its positive\nendpoint contributes the third term.\n\nThe formal middle term does not create a spurious extremum when R<1:\nd*R-h*v <= (h*kappa-1)*R, since their difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.\nIts positive part is therefore bounded by the actual core term.\nIf R<u, a positive formal tail value satisfies\nR-h*B<=R-h*G(R), the actual positive endpoint value, which is already\ncovered by the core or middle endpoint. This also handles R=0.\n\nThe signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0. Its image of [-R,R]\nis the entire symmetric interval with radius (3.1). Thus exact\nchronological composition yields\n\n    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(h_j^lev) composed with M_j. (3.2)\n\nNo independently chosen incompatible child start is used. The quantitative\nresult below does not rely on a numerical replay of this identity.\n\n## 4. Same-m masses and the core prefix lemma\n\nFor all m in the assignment,\n\n    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>99/100,\n    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,\n    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2,\n    c*theta=beta^2<1.                                 (4.1)\n\nUse 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 for the c bounds. The lower a bound follows\nfrom a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5. For theta use\ntheta=beta^2/c>(99/100)^2*(7/17)>2/5.\nThe other inequalities follow directly.\n\nPut eta=a/(c-2). Since lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda,\n\n    0<eta<1,\n    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,\n    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                           (4.2)\n\nThe formula is verified by substitution and A_0=1. In particular,\n\n    A_j>d_j/(c-2),    A_j<c^j for j>=1,\n    D=2A_m-1<2c^m=2d_m/a.                            (4.3)\n\nAlso for every j>=1,\n\n    S_j/d_j>7/5.                                      (4.4)\n\nAt j=1 the ratio is ell/beta>7/5. If L=S_j/d_j>7/5,\nthe next ratio is (2L+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(5/2)>7/5.\nThis is a symbolic induction, not an executed recurrence.\n\nDefine P_0=1 and\nP_j=product_(h in H_j)|1-kappa*h| for j>=1.\nFor every 0<kappa<=1, jointly,\n\n    every physical prefix product of H_j is at most1,\n    P_j<=1,\n    kappa*d_j*P_j<=theta for j>=1.                    (4.5)\n\nFor the leaf, |1-ell*kappa|<=1. Moreover\nkappa*|1-ell*kappa|<=a:\non kappa<=1/ell its maximum is 1/(4ell)<a;\non kappa>=1/ell, kappa*(ell*kappa-1) increases up to a at1.\nMultiplication by d_1=beta gives the last bound.\n\nSuppose the claims are known for H_j, j>=1. If the level-j core\nbridge reverses, its magnitude is\n|1-kappa*(1+d_j)|<=kappa*d_j. Therefore\n\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n      <=c*(kappa*d_j*P_j)^2\n      <=c*theta^2=theta*beta^2<theta.\n\nThe prefix ending at the bridge has multiplier at most\nkappa*d_j*P_j<=theta<1. Multiplying it by any prefix product of\nthe right H_j preserves the prefix bound.\n\nIf that bridge is nonreversing, every effective core step in H_(j+1)\nbelongs to [0,1]. Its prefix products are at most1, and\n\n    P_(j+1)<=exp(-kappa*S_(j+1)),\n    kappa*d_(j+1)*P_(j+1)\n      <=1/[exp(1)*(S_(j+1)/d_(j+1))]\n      <5/14<theta.\n\nHere z*exp(-Lz)<=1/[exp(1)*L] follows by differentiation.\nThese arguments include zero factors and equality at a reversal\nthreshold, and prove (4.5).\n\nConsequently a complete H_j started anywhere inside the closed core\nremains there at EVERY physical prefix. Its actual output radius is\nP_j times its input radius. This statement concerns a complete child,\nnot an arbitrary suffix begun at an arbitrary physical index.\n\n## 5. The actual first-crossing price in a nonreversing child\n\nConsider any complete H_b with kappa*(1+d_(b-1))<=1; for b=0 there\nare no steps. Core entry is absorbing in H_b.\n\nSuppose an actual trajectory in H_b has a first strict sign crossing,\nat a level-k physical step h=1+d_k. Let r be its source radius,\np=G(r), and P its actual original prefix mass inside H_b.\nCore absorption forces r>1. For every exterior source,\n\n    r>=p+v.\n\nBefore the first crossing all radii decrease, and every queried gradient\nis at least p. The complete left H_k immediately preceding this occurrence\ngives P>=S_k. Therefore, for the original radius R_b of this child,\n\n    R_b>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)*p.\n\nStrict crossing means (1+d_k)*p>r>=p+v, so p>v/d_k. It follows that\n\n    R_b>v*(1+(1+P)/d_k)\n        >=v*(1+A_k/d_k)>v Q. It does not reset that denominator.\n\nThe same proof also gives a local no-crossing criterion used below:\na start with radius at most v Q cannot have a strict crossing in H_J.\nA zero hit is absorbing.\n\n## 6. The first reversing bridge and its complete right child\n\nFrom the definition of positive J,\n\n    kappa*(1+d_(J-1))<=1,\n    kappa*(1+d_J)>1,\n    v>=a/(1+a)>2/7,\n    v<t<=c*v,\n    0<q=t-v<3v/2,\n    q<(3/5)*t,\n    Q>3,  L_J>7/5.                                   (6.1)\n\nThe bound on v uses J>=1 and hence kappa*ell<=1.\nThe t upper bound follows by multiplying\nkappa*d_(J-1)<=v by c. The q/t bound follows from q<3v/2.\n\nAssume the initial H_J has actual terminal radius y<=1.\nIts level-J bridge has actual landing radius z=q*y<=q, and the\nsign reverses unless y=0. No core approximation is made if z>1.\n\nThe following complete H_J, begun at z, has NO strict crossing:\nits own core is absorbing, while an exterior first crossing would\nrequire z>v Q by (5.1), contrary to z<=q<3v/2<v Q.\nIt must enter the core by its actual terminal. Otherwise all queried\nand terminal radii exceed1 and every gradient is at least kappa,\nso its final radius would be at most\n\n    z-kappa*S_J<=q-L_J*t<0,\n\na contradiction. A same-side terminal core entry is included here.\n\nTo bound the returned radius, use the scalar comparison update\n\n    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1).\n\nThis comparison map is nonnegative and nondecreasing on [0,infinity)\nbecause every effective step of H_J has h*kappa<=1. Original setting and uniform full-depth guards\n\nFor a fixed nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum of\n2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite Euclidean dimensions,\nconvex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimum, a minimizer\nx_star, x_0!=x_star, and the original updates\nx_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Nothing in this report proves\nor disproves that inequality.\n\nFix an integer m>=1536 and use these SAME parameters at every child:\n\n    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), h=H_m,\n    n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S_j=sum H_j, S=S_m=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,\n    q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q), b=1+a, g=1+beta,\n    H=1+a*c^2=1+(1+sqrt(2))(1-eps^2).\n\nThe scalar H without a subscript is ONLY the middle physical step in the\nlast H_3 word.
